Um, well, I guess... I mean, I don't really hide, exactly. It's just that, you know, if my roommates are hanging out in the kitchen, I'll just grab a normal-ish amount of food, like anyone else would. Just a regular snack or whatever.

And, um, I do spend a lot of time worrying if my roommates noticed how much I ate, which makes me feel kind of embarrassed. But it's mostly just me being in my own head about it, you know? I hope I'm not making it sound worse than it is.\n\nDoctor: When these episodes happen, do you find yourself eating much more rapidly than usual?\n\nPatient: Um, yeah, I guess I do. I mean, I know some people do that kind of stuff, but I would never make myself throw up or take pills or anything crazy. \n\nI might, you know, just try to eat a little less the next day or maybe try to hit the gym if I have time between classes, but nothing extreme. Just normal stuff to kind of balance it out, I guess? I hope that's the right answer... I just want to make sure you know I'm not doing anything dangerous or anything like that. Is that what you were asking?\n\nDoctor: Do these episodes of eating happen even when you’re not really hungry, or do you usually feel hungry before they start?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I mean, I'm still making it to all my classes fine, and I still go to my robotics club meetings, so it's not like it's ruining my life or anything. \n\nBut, I guess... it does bother me a little bit. Sometimes the morning after it happens, I just feel kind of sluggish, or maybe a bit distracted. And, um, I do spend a lot of time worrying if my roommates noticed how much I ate, which makes me feel kind of embarrassed. But it's mostly just me being in my own head about it, you know? It hasn't actually knocked me off track with school or anything. Does that make sense? I hope I'm not making it sound worse than it is.\n\nDoctor: When these episodes happen, do you find yourself eating much more rapidly than usual?\n\nPatient: Um, yeah, I guess I do. But I think it's mostly just because I'm trying to multitask, you know? Like, during those times, I'll just be standing at my desk, trying to squeeze something to eat in between finishing up my homework and hopping into a game. \n\nSo I do eat pretty fast then. Honestly, I'll barely even taste it, and then I'll suddenly realize all the food is just... gone, in only a few minutes. I know that might sound a little bad, but I think a lot of guys my age eat fast when they're distracted or in a hurry, right? I hope that answers what you were asking.\n\nDoctor: After these episodes of eating, do you ever try to compensate for what you ate, like making yourself vomit, using laxatives, or doing something else to try to 'undo' the eating?\n\nPatient: Um, no, I... I definitely don't do anything like that. I mean, I know some people do that kind of stuff, but I would never make myself throw up or take pills or anything crazy. \n\nI might, you know, just try to eat a little less the next day or maybe try to hit the gym if I have time between classes, but nothing extreme. Just normal stuff to kind of balance it out, I guess? I hope that's the right answer... I just want to make sure you know I'm not doing anything dangerous or anything like that. Is that what you were asking?\n\nDoctor: Do these episodes of eating happen even when you’re not really hungry, or do you usually feel hungry before they start?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I guess it kind of depends? Like, sometimes I'm probably a little hungry, since I'm usually up late studying or gaming, and you know how it is when you're up late. But, um... honestly, a lot of the time I don't think I'm actually, like, physically hungry. \n\nIt's more just that the food is there, or I'm feeling kind of stressed about a project, and I just start eating without really thinking about if my stomach is growling or anything. I mean, I think it's pretty normal to snack when you're stressed or bored, right? It's not like I'm eating because I'm starving, but it just kind of happens. Does that make sense? I hope I'm explaining it right.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself eating alone during these episodes because you're embarrassed about how much you're eating?\n\nPatient: Um, well, I guess... I mean, I don't really hide, exactly. It's just that, you know, if my roommates are hanging out in the kitchen, I'll just grab a normal-ish amount of food, like anyone else would. Just a regular snack or whatever. \n\nBut then, um... sometimes, later on, I might just sort of take some extra stuff back to my room with me. Not because it's a huge deal, but just... I don't really want them seeing how much I'm actually eating, you know? Guys can be kind of judgmental about that stuff, and I don't want them making jokes or thinking I'm weird. So it's just easier to eat in my room while I'm studying or whatever. That's pretty normal for college guys, right? Just wanting a little privacy when you eat? I hope that doesn't sound too strange.\n\nDoctor: How often do these episodes happen, and how long have you been experiencing them?\n\nPatient: Um, well... let's see. I guess it started pretty early on in the semester? It's not like an everyday thing, I promise. It's usually just, like, maybe one night a week? Sometimes two, if things get really crazy with midterms or if there's just a lot of junk food lying around the apartment. \n\nI'd say it's been going on like that for... maybe the last three or four months? Basically since the semester really got going. But it's not like I'm doing it all the time, just when things get a little stressful. That's not too bad, right? I mean, I feel like a lot of guys probably do the same thing when they're stressed with classes. Is that... is that kind of what you were looking for?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel distressed or upset about these eating episodes, even if they don’t interfere with your school or social life?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I mean, I wouldn't say I'm, like, totally devastated or anything. It's not ruining my life, you know? But, um... yeah, I guess I do feel a little... I don't know, embarrassed afterward? \n\nIt's just that I'm usually really on top of things. Like with my classes and my robotics team, I'm super disciplined. So when I do this, it just makes me feel kind of... grossed out with myself, I guess? Like I'm just sitting there and I realize I wasn't really in control for a minute. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },

    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um, well, I wouldn't say I get *sick* or anything like that. I know some people really push it too far, but I don't think I'm that bad. 

But, um, I guess I do keep going until I feel... really full. Like, my stomach gets kind of tight and heavy? Sometimes it gets to the point where I just need to go lie down on my bed for a bit because sitting up at my desk is just too uncomfortable. 

I mean, I definitely wish I'd stopped sooner when I feel like that, but I'm just enjoying the food, you know? It's not like I'm trying to make myself miserable on purpose. I hope that makes sense. Is it pretty common for guys my age to eat until they're stuffed like that?

I hope that answers what you were asking.\n\nDoctor: After these episodes of eating, do you ever try to compensate for what you ate, like making yourself vomit, using laxatives, or doing something else to try to 'undo' the eating?\n\nPatient: Um, no, I... I definitely don't do anything like that. I mean, I know some people do that kind of stuff, but I would never make myself throw up or take pills or anything crazy. \n\nI might, you know, just try to eat a little less the next day or maybe try to hit the gym if I have time between classes, but nothing extreme. Just normal stuff to kind of balance it out, I guess? I hope that's the right answer... I just want to make sure you know I'm not doing anything dangerous or anything like that. Is that what you were asking?\n\nDoctor: Do these episodes of eating happen even when you’re not really hungry, or do you usually feel hungry before they start?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I guess it kind of depends? Like, sometimes I'm probably a little hungry, since I'm usually up late studying or gaming, and you know how it is when you're up late. But, um... honestly, a lot of the time I don't think I'm actually, like, physically hungry. \n\nIt's more just that the food is there, or I'm feeling kind of stressed about a project, and I just start eating without really thinking about if my stomach is growling or anything. I mean, I think it's pretty normal to snack when you're stressed or bored, right? It's not like I'm eating because I'm starving, but it just kind of happens. Does that make sense? I hope I'm explaining it right.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself eating alone during these episodes because you're embarrassed about how much you're eating?\n\nPatient: Um, well, I guess... I mean, I don't really hide, exactly. It's just that, you know, if my roommates are hanging out in the kitchen, I'll just grab a normal-ish amount of food, like anyone else would. Just a regular snack or whatever. \n\nBut then, um... sometimes, later on, I might just sort of take some extra stuff back to my room with me. Not because it's a huge deal, but just... I don't really want them seeing how much I'm actually eating, you know? Guys can be kind of judgmental about that stuff, and I don't want them making jokes or thinking I'm weird. So it's just easier to eat in my room while I'm studying or whatever. That's pretty normal for college guys, right? Just wanting a little privacy when you eat? I hope that doesn't sound too strange.\n\nDoctor: How often do these episodes happen, and how long have you been experiencing them?\n\nPatient: Um, well... let's see. I guess it started pretty early on in the semester? It's not like an everyday thing, I promise. It's usually just, like, maybe one night a week? Sometimes two, if things get really crazy with midterms or if there's just a lot of junk food lying around the apartment. \n\nI'd say it's been going on like that for... maybe the last three or four months? Basically since the semester really got going. But it's not like I'm doing it all the time, just when things get a little stressful. That's not too bad, right? I mean, I feel like a lot of guys probably do the same thing when they're stressed with classes. Is that... is that kind of what you were looking for?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel distressed or upset about these eating episodes, even if they don’t interfere with your school or social life?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I mean, I wouldn't say I'm, like, totally devastated or anything. It's not ruining my life, you know? But, um... yeah, I guess I do feel a little... I don't know, embarrassed afterward? \n\nIt's just that I'm usually really on top of things. Like with my classes and my robotics team, I'm super disciplined. So when I do this, it just makes me feel kind of... grossed out with myself, I guess? Like I'm just sitting there and I realize I wasn't really in control for a minute. But I mean, everyone has moments where they slip up a little, right? It's not like a huge deal, but it does bother me a bit. Does that make sense? I hope I'm not making it sound worse than it actually is.\n\nDoctor: When you're eating during these episodes, do you feel like you're not in control of your eating, like you can't stop once you start, even if you wanted to?\n\nPatient: Um, well, I guess that's... that's kind of the part that made me finally decide to make an appointment. I mean, it's not like I'm totally out of my mind or anything, but... yeah, sometimes it feels like I hit this weird switch. \n\nLike, I'll be eating, and part of my brain is just sitting there going, \"Dennis, come on man, just stop already. You don't need another sleeve of cookies.\" But then I just... I kind of keep going anyway? It's not like I'm totally out of control, just... maybe a little less in control than I'd like to be in that moment. I know that probably sounds a little weak, right? Like I should just be able to stop. I hope you don't think I'm just making excuses for bad habits. Does that sound like what you're asking about?\n\nDoctor: When you're in the middle of one of these episodes, would you say that you feel a complete lack of control over how much you're eating, like you can't stop eating even though you want to?\n\nPatient: Yeah, um, exactly... I guess, like I was just trying to say, that's really what made me come in to see you. It's just that once I start eating like that, it really feels like I hit some switch. And I keep going even while a part of me is literally thinking, Dennis, just stop already. \n\nI guess I hesitate to use the word complete, just because that makes it sound so... I don't know, extreme? Like I'm totally helpless or something, and I don't want to sound like I'm completely falling apart. But, um, yeah, the truth is I really can't seem to stop even when I want to. Does that answer it a little better? I hope I'm making sense this time.\n\nDoctor: Do these episodes cause you significant distress or affect any areas of your life, like your mood, focus, or motivation, even if they don’t seem to interfere with your schoolwork?\n\nPatient: Um, well, like I said, it's not really messing up my life or anything. I'm still making it to all my classes and showing up for robotics, so I'm not, like, failing or falling behind. \n\nBut, um... I guess maybe it does affect me a little bit? Like, sometimes the next morning I feel kind of sluggish, or I might be a little distracted thinking about it. And I probably spend a little too much time worrying if my roommates noticed anything, which is kind of silly, I know. \n\nSo it's not really knocking me off track, but it definitely bothers me more than I'd like to admit. I just try not to let it show, you know? I hope that doesn't make me sound like I'm too obsessed with what other people think. Is that the kind of thing you're asking about?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself eating alone during these episodes because you're embarrassed about how much you're eating?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I guess I do try to keep it kind of private, yeah. It's not that I'm, like, hiding in a closet or anything weird like that. It's just... \n\nIf my roommates are hanging out in the kitchen, I'll just, you know, eat a normal-ish amount. Like a regular snack or whatever so I don't look weird. But then later, I might grab some extra stuff and just take it back to my room. It's just easier that way, I guess? I just don't really want them seeing how much I'm actually eating, because... well, guys can be kind of brutal about that stuff, and I don't want them making jokes or thinking I'm gross. \n\nIs that normal to do? I mean, I'm sure lots of people prefer to eat snacks in their room, right? I hope that doesn't make me sound super sneaky or anything.\n\nDoctor: When you're eating during these episodes, do you usually keep eating until you feel really uncomfortably full, even if part of you wishes you had stopped earlier?\n\nPatient: Um, well, I wouldn't say I get *sick* or anything like that. I know some people really push it too far, but I don't think I'm that bad. \n\nBut, um, I guess I do keep going until I feel... really full. Like, my stomach gets kind of tight and heavy? Sometimes it gets to the point where I just need to go lie down on my bed for a bit because sitting up at my desk is just too uncomfortable. \n\nI mean, I definitely wish I'd stopped sooner when I feel like that, but I'm just enjoying the food, you know? It's not like I'm trying to make myself miserable on purpose. I hope that makes sense. Is it pretty common for guys my age to eat until they're stuffed like that?\n\nDoctor: Do these episodes of eating happen within a short period of time, like a couple of hours, where you're focused on eating and it feels like a single episode rather than something that's spread out over the whole day?\n\nPatient: Um, well, yeah, I guess it's usually just in one sitting. It's not like I'm doing it all day long or anything crazy like that. \n\nLike, if I've just finished a big project for a class, or if there's, you know, leftover pizza sitting around the apartment, I might grab it and take it to my room. And then... well, I might end up eating, like, a whole small pizza, and maybe some chips and cereal too. All kind of at once, over an hour or two in the evening. \n\nIt's definitely more than I originally planned to eat, and probably way more than my roommates would have for just a regular dinner. But it's just when I'm trying to unwind, you know? I hope that doesn't make me sound completely out of control. Do you think that's a pretty normal way to blow off steam after a deadline?"
